<td valign="top"><strong>Question:</strong> Which biological process best describes the pathway in which this protein participates?<br><br><strong>Choices:</strong><br><br><strong>A.</strong> Pyrimidine nucleotide biosynthetic process<br><br><strong>B.</strong> Purine nucleotide salvage process<br><br><strong>C.</strong> De novo IMP biosynthetic process<br><br><strong>D.</strong> Purine nucleotide degradation process<br><br><strong>Flash-Next response:</strong><br><br><br><br>1. reasoning: Analyzing the protein sequence, I can identify this as ATIC (5-aminoimidazole-4-carboxamide ribonucleotide formyltransferase/IMP cyclohydrolase), a bifunctional enzyme. Key observations:<br><br>- The protein contains distinct domain architecture with N-terminal features associated with AIR carboxylase activity and C-terminal features associated with SAICAR synthetase activity (and formyltransferase/cyclohydrolase in the vertebrate bifunctional form).<br>- The sequence length (~600 aa) and domain organization are consistent with ATIC.<br>- ATIC catalyzes steps 6 and 7 (and in vertebrates, steps 9 and 10) of the de novo purine biosynthesis pathway, which ultimately produces IMP (inosine monophosphate).<br><br>Evaluating each option:<br>- A (Pyrimidine nucleotide biosynthesis): Incorrect. ATIC is involved in purine, not pyrimidine, biosynthesis.<br>- B (Purine nucleotide salvage): Incorrect. Salvage pathways recycle pre-formed bases/nucleosides; ATIC is involved in de novo synthesis from scratch.<br>- C (De novo IMP biosynthetic process): Correct. ATIC is a central enzyme in the 10-step de novo IMP biosynthetic pathway.<br>- D (Purine nucleotide degradation): Incorrect. ATIC participates in synthesis, not degradation.<br><br>2. answer: C</td>
<td valign="top"><strong>Question:</strong> For the protein represented by this sequence, which biological process best describes its pathway participation? Distinguish among the alternatives by matching its sequence-inferred enzymatic role to the pathway step it supports, rather than treating related nucleotide processes as interchangeable.<br><br><strong>Choices:</strong><br><br><strong>A.</strong> Pyrimidine nucleotide biosynthetic process<br><br><strong>B.</strong> Purine nucleotide salvage process<br><br><strong>C.</strong> De novo IMP biosynthetic process<br><br><strong>D.</strong> Purine nucleotide degradation process<br><br><strong>Skills:</strong> GO_biological_process_0005:2<br><br><strong>Flash-Next response:</strong><br><br><br><br>1. reasoning: <br><br>**Sequence Analysis:**<br>The protein sequence contains several key diagnostic motifs:<br>- The motif **&quot;GAGIVDIGDNQAVVFKI&quot;** is the hallmark of a **glutamine amidotransferase (GAT) class I domain**, specifically the type found in GMP synthase (GuaA/GMPS, EC 6.3.5.2).<br>- The region **&quot;GAKTGRDGIHGATF&quot;** is consistent with the **ATP pyrophosphatase domain** of GMP synthase.<br>- The C-terminal region contains features of an **amidase-type ATP-grasp domain**.<br><br>**Functional Identification:**<br>This protein is **GMP synthase**, which catalyzes:<br>XMP + ATP + Glutamine + H₂O → GMP + AMP + PPi + Glutamate<br><br>This is the final ATP-dependent amination step converting XMP (derived from IMP via IMP dehydrogenase) to GMP.<br><br>**Evaluating the Options:**<br>- **A. Pyrimidine nucleotide biosynthetic process** — Incorrect. Pyrimidine biosynthesis involves CAD, DHODH, and CPS. No structural resemblance.<br>- **B. Purine nucleotide salvage process** — Incorrect. Salvage involves phosphoribosyltransferases (HGPRT, APRT) that directly add PRPP to free bases. No resemblance to this sequence.<br>- **C. De novo IMP biosynthetic process** — This is the best match. GMP synthase operates in the **de novo purine nucleotide biosynthesis pathway**, specifically downstream of IMP (IMP → XMP → GMP). The question&#x27;s hint about &quot;matching its sequence-inferred enzymatic role to the pathway step it supports&quot; and &quot;not treating related nucleotide processes as interchangeable&quot; points to this being the de novo purine biosynthesis pathway.<br>- **D. Purine nucleotide degradation process** — Incorrect. Degradation involves xanthine oxidase, purine nucleoside phosphorylase, uricase, etc. GMP synthase works in the biosynthetic direction.<br><br>The answer is C because GMP synthase is a committed step in the de novo purine nucleotide biosynthetic pathway (from IMP through to GMP).<br><br>2. answer: C</td>

The answer is C.
